# Known issues register

From the full production review of 2026-08-05 (see [`../sessions/2026-08-05.md`](../sessions/2026-08-05.md)).
IDs are stable — reference them in commits and session logs.

Status: `open` · `in-progress` · `fixed` · `wontfix` · `dormant` (real defect, not currently
reachable with default config — will bite when config changes) · `partly fixed` / `partly
unblocked` (one hop or cluster done, rest open — the entry says which) · `not a defect / partly
invalid` (the finding itself was wrong; kept so the correction is discoverable)

Nothing here is speculative; each entry cites the code. Three entries are explicitly marked
**unverified** where I could not exercise the behaviour.

---

## Critical

| ID | Issue | Location | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | Operator precedence: `RightControl \|\| (LeftControl && Alt && R)` — Right Ctrl **alone** triggers `FullRestart`, and `GetKey` not `GetKeyDown` re-fires it every frame, spawning dozens of processes | `MainController.cs` `Update`/`FullRestart` (was :102) | **fixed 2026-08-06** (v0.1.1) |
| C2 | Asset cleanup recursively deleted every file under a **computed** root that wasn't in `mediaAssets`. Root-side failures: an empty `assetsPath` collapsed the root to streamingAssets/persistentData themselves; symlink/junction roots escaped containment; the editor root is project source. Keep-set-side failures (all found by review, none by testing): `GetAssetPath` does `string.Concat` with **no separator**, so an `assetsPath` without a trailing slash produced `.../assetsvideo.mp4` and matched nothing; it returns **rooted** values as-is, so a CMS emitting `/video.mp4` yielded `C:\video.mp4` and the real file was deleted; the `mediaPaths` allowlist and the properties-only reflection walk (M3/M4) both silently shrink the list. Plus a case-sensitive ordinal `==`, no `try/catch` on `File.Delete` (one locked file killed the coroutine and hung boot mid-delete), and it rode on `AssetDownload.isActive` so enabling downloads enabled a delete. Now: dedicated `cleanUnlistedAssets` opt-in (default false); root must be a strict subfolder of **persistentDataPath only** and not a reparse point; keep-set built with `Path.Combine` against the root and **refuses entirely unless at least one entry resolves under it**; **refuses unless ≥50% of the keep-list is actually present on disk** (corroboration - see M29 for why the original 50%-of-folder volume cap was wrong); emptied folders removed deepest-first; per-file `try/catch`; completion event on every exit path; O(n) instead of O(n·m)-with-a-Regex | `AssetManager.cs` `ValidateExistingMedia`/`IsSafeCleanupRoot`/`IsUnder`/`ProcessFiles_IE` | **fixed 2026-08-07** (v0.7.0, revised v0.8.1), flag off by default |
| C3 | Boot is a chain of one-shot events with no timeout, so any link that never fires leaves the app on the preloader forever with nothing surfaced. Now watched end to end: every hop calls `Core.MarkBootStage`, and a coroutine started at the top of `Core.Awake` - before the settings parse, so it covers that failure too - fires when no stage changes for a stall budget, logs at **Error**, shows a notification, latches `_bootFailed` so `LoadNextScene` refuses to start with incomplete data, and keeps watching so a later stall is reported too. **Stall, not total**: a legitimate first-run download outlasts any fixed budget, so per-asset progress marks feed it - without those, two assets hitting the request timeout tripped it on a healthy boot. **Regressed once and fixed in v0.15.1:** the per-asset call was wrapped in `if (AssetDownload.doLogs)` to stop 200 lines of progress spam, which is a fair thing to want but silently removed the deadline resets, since `doLogs` ships `false`. `MarkBootStage` now takes `(stage, log)` so the reset cannot be opted out of - the same mistake is no longer expressible at a call site. The budget is `max(90s, requestTimeout * 2)` because the longest gap a healthy boot leaves between marks is one network request. `_bootComplete` is set immediately before `SceneManager.LoadScene` at all three call sites, **not** on entry to `LoadNextScene` - with `UseAppInfo` the real scene load is several unguarded callbacks later (a LeanTween completion, and a `CoreSystemUI.Show()` that silently does nothing if `_appInfo` is null), so marking it early disarmed the watchdog immediately before the one hop it most needed to watch | `Core.cs` `MarkBootStage`/`BootWatchdog_IE`/`MarkBootComplete`, hop marks in `MainController.cs` | **fixed 2026-08-10** (v0.10.0) |
| C4 | Hardcoded `"\\"` / `"config\\"` path separators — config, asset cache and `SaveAppSettings` all broken on Android/iOS/Linux/macOS. Root cause was one value with a separator baked in (`FileManager.configDirectory = "config\\"`), which broke even the sites that already used `Path.Combine` — on Linux they produced a directory literally named `config\` | now `AppPaths` (`Core/Managers/AppPaths.cs`); migrated `FileManager`, `AppDataSettings`, `AppDataController`, `ConfigManager`, `MainController`, `BatchFileCreator` | **fixed 2026-08-07** (v0.3.0) — compiles clean (confirmed by Tiago in the Editor; `Editor.log` 15:05 shows 0 `error CS`). Android runtime-verified 2026-08-14 - the config folder and the persistent asset cache were both composed and used during a device boot; iOS/macOS/Linux still unexercised |
| C5 | `ActionExtensions.InvokeLater` ran the callback on a `System.Timers.Timer` thread-pool thread, so any Unity API inside it threw; no cancellation, so a pending one fired into destroyed objects. **Deleted**, both overloads, rather than fixed: they had zero callers, deletion is the smaller change, and it stops the next project reaching for a helper whose name gives no hint that it cannot work. Use a coroutine. **Breaking change for branched projects** that call it - it fails at compile time, which is loud, but it will fail | `ActionExtensions.cs` | **fixed 2026-08-10** (v0.9.0) |
| C6 | `SocketConnection` rewritten (v0.20.0). The old design was four interlocking defects, not four independent ones: a throwaway thread that connected and then invoked a `UnityEvent` from itself, per-frame `DataAvailable` polling on the main thread, three unsynchronised `bool`s shared between the two, and `SendTimeout = 3` **milliseconds**.<br><br>**Now:** one worker thread owns the socket - connect via `BeginConnect` with a 5&#160;s timeout, then a blocking read loop that frames bytes on `'\|'` into a `ConcurrentQueue<string>`. The worker touches no Unity API at all; every event, log and notification is raised from `Update`. One `volatile bool` replaces the three flags, and the two serialized bools are display mirrors read by nothing. `SendTimeout` is 3000. `CloseSocket` is idempotent and null-safe, with `OnDestroy`/`OnApplicationQuit`.<br><br>**The leak's mechanism, since the old entry only asserted it:** `new TcpClient(host, port)` has no connect timeout - about 21&#160;s to an unreachable host on Windows - while the reconnect fired every `reconnectTime`, 10&#160;s as shipped. Attempts therefore overlapped, each overwriting the socket field without closing it, and `CloseSocket`'s `if (!serverReady) return;` made it a no-op on precisely the failure path that called it. A handle every 10&#160;s, indefinitely.<br><br>**Five regressions in the rewrite, three found by review and two by running it. One was worse than the bug it replaced.** (1) The reconnect branch did not tear down first, so a half-open socket - pulled cable, powered-off host, no FIN or RST - left the worker blocked in `Read` forever; `JoinWorker` then froze the **main thread** for its full 6&#160;s timeout, abandoned a live thread, and the abandoned worker later reported the by-then *healthy* connection as dead, compounding one leaked thread per network blip. (2) `_reportedState` started at `-1`, so the first `Update` spent the only transition slot on a failure that had not happened - a kiosk-visible error notification at boot, `onDisconnectedEvent` raised to child code, and every real connect failure thereafter reported to nobody. The old code logged every attempt, so that was a **regression in observability**. (3) A dropped-message overflow was reported through `_lastError`, which is only read on a disconnect - so losing messages while connected was silent, and the warning later resurfaced pretending to be the reason the connection died. (4) **Stopping play logged `Failed connecting to OneCore Server` as an error, every time.** `OnApplicationQuit` closed the socket, `CloseSocket` raised its transition, and `OneCore`'s handler reports any disconnection as a failed connection - so a deliberate shutdown was reported as a connection failure. Nothing subscribed to that event can usefully run during quit, so the transition is now suppressed while quitting. `code-reviewer` had cleared this exact path by verifying that the *second* `CloseSocket` fires nothing; the defect was that the first one fires anything. Found by Tiago on the first run.<br><br>**Also corrected while there:** `OneCore.OnConnectionChanged` reported every disconnection as "Failed connecting", which sent people to check a server address that had been fine for hours. It now distinguishes a lost connection from a failed connect.<br><br>(5) **Merging the two classes made the OneCore connection announce itself as "Socket Server".** The deleted `OneCoreSocketConnection` had said "OneCore Server"; one shared class cannot know which server it is talking to, so it now takes a `connectionName` its owner sets. The merge had also added a success notification the OneCore path never had, on top of `OneCore`'s own - three toasts for one connection, two of them saying the same thing under the wrong name. `OneCore`'s duplicates are removed rather than the shared ones: `SocketConnection` carries the reason, which `OneCore`'s never did. Its `onConnectedEvent` and `onDisconnectedEvent` are untouched - the events are the API, the toasts were not. Also found by Tiago, from the screen rather than the log.<br><br>**Stated limits.** No `SO_KEEPALIVE` and no `ReceiveTimeout`: a blocking read on a half-open socket never returns, so the keep-alive **ping is the only liveness detector**. That is why `keepAliveTime` and `reconnectTime` now have an upper bound as well as a lower one (`SocketsSettings.MaxIntervalSeconds`, 300) - unbounded, either one silently disabled recovery while still reading as connected. **Untested against a real server:** both `isActive` flags ship `false`, and this repo has no Gema Core host. The framing logic was proven out-of-tree against `csc.exe` with 13 cases (several messages per read, a message split across reads, a UTF-8 character split across a read boundary, withheld partials, CRLF stripping, empty frames, honouring the read count) | `SocketConnection.cs`, `SocketManager.cs:61-80`, `OneCore.cs:186-215`, `SocketsSettings.cs:33,65,68` | **fixed 2026-08-12** (v0.20.0) |
| C7 | `[Conditional("DO_LOGS")]` on `LogError`/`LogException` removed the **call sites**, and `DO_LOGS` is defined only for Android + Standalone — so iOS, WebGL, macOS, Linux and UWP builds reported nothing at all. The 4 `LogError` overloads and `LogException` are now always compiled (~129 call sites start emitting); verbose `Log`/`LogWarning`/`CheckPoint`/`Break` stay conditional. **`LogIf` (then `Assert`) deliberately stays conditional** — its arguments are stripped with the call and 73 of the 77 sites pass `Core.settings.<Module>.doLogs` (the other 4 pass a local field), so making it unconditional would spread H17's NRE to every platform. Verified none of the 77 passes `LogType.Error`, so it costs no error reporting. (Counts are as of v0.5.0; 69 sites as of v0.41.0, still none passing `LogType.Error`) | `Development.cs` | **fixed 2026-08-07** (v0.5.0) |
| C8 | `AppDataSettings.SetData` key mismatch: **3 of the 9 AppData fields in the settings panel never saved.** `case "assetsPath"` never matched (the scene authors `remoteAssetsPath`, `CoreSetup.unity:18143`) and additionally assigned from the computed `assetsPath` *property* rather than `value`; `localAssetsPath` (`:8601`) and `localDataJsonPath` (`:6894`) had no case at all and fell through a silent `default`. Operator edits those three fields, saves, nothing happens, no error | `AppDataSettings.cs` `SetData` | **fixed 2026-08-06** (v0.1.2) |

## High

| ID | Issue | Location | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | Two unrelated singleton defects. (a) `MainController.Instance` was a **public instance** field, so `Awake`'s `if (Instance != null)` read a fresh component's own field and could never be true - the duplicate guard was unreachable - and being public it was **serialized and offered in the inspector**, so anyone filling that slot would have made it destroy itself on the next launch (the scene had it empty, so the trap was never armed). Now `private static`, and the `DestroyImmediate(this)` + `throw` - which threw out of `Awake` from an object it had just destroyed - is `enabled = false; Destroy(this);`. The `enabled = false` matters: `Destroy` is deferred to end of frame while `Core` resolves `main` via `GetComponentInChildren` with undefined Awake ordering, so Core could otherwise bind to the copy that is about to die and call `Init()` on it. (b) `MainThreadDispatcher.Instance` was a static with no `OnDestroy`. Adding one exposed a **TOCTOU that the fix itself created**: `RunOnMainThread` read the static three times, so a worker could pass the null guard and then dereference null on the lock - on a socket thread, where the exception is swallowed. It is snapshotted into a local now, tested with `ReferenceEquals` rather than Unity's `==` (which reads native state and is not safe off the main thread), and reports at Error instead of throwing | `MainController.cs` `Awake`, `MainThreadDispatcher.cs` | **fixed 2026-08-10** (v0.12.0) |
| H2 | `SetupXxx` helpers `return` early when the component already exists, skipping `Init()` and event subscription. Currently **dormant** — but the reason was re-derived on 2026-08-13 and the old one was wrong. It used to read "none of the affected components are on `Core.prefab`", a prefab nothing referenced and which is now deleted (M74); mapped against `CoreSetup.unity`, the scene that actually boots, `AssetManager` and `TextureCacheManager` **are** present, so the adopt path does run for them - it is just that both of their helpers subscribe unconditionally and have no early `return`. The **nine** that do - `SetupSerialPorts`, `SetupForegroundEnforcer`, `SetupDebugConsole`, `SetupOscController`, `SetupApiController`, `SetupSocketManager`, `SetupActivityMonitor`, `SetupPhidgets`, `SetupOneCore` - all serve components absent from the scene; `SerialPortsManager` and `OSCController` are the worked examples. Becomes live the moment either is added to `CoreSetup.unity`. Live examples: `SetupSerialPorts:454-467`, `SetupOscController:684-697` (**not** `SetupTextureCacheManager`, which was fixed in v0.2.0 and is now the correct pattern to copy)<br><br>**Fixed, and the survey shrank it from nine sites to three that mattered.** All nine had the shape - adopt, then `return` - but six of them had nothing after the return except the creation lines the adopt path legitimately skips. The three that lost real work: `SetupDebugConsole` skipped every console setting (toggle key, log cap, shake-to-open); `SetupApiController` skipped `Api.Init()`; and worst, `SetupOscController` skipped **`CreateClient`**, so an authored `OSCController` would have been adopted and then never given a client - OSC silently doing nothing, which is H2's failure mode exactly.<br><br>All nine were restructured anyway, to `if (X == null) { create }` followed by unconditional init, because the *shape* is the trap: leaving six of them adopt-then-return means the next person to add an init line after one gets a silent no-op for adopted components. The three that dereference now carry the `LogError` guard `SetupTextureCacheManager` already had, and `AddComponent`'s return value replaces a redundant `GetComponent` read.<br><br>Done as one regex transform with a hard count assert rather than nine hand edits - which earned itself immediately: it matched **8**, not 9, because `SetupOneCore` lacks a blank line the others have. A hand pass is where that one gets missed.<br><br>Verified by driving `SetupDebugConsole` on a MainController assembled in edit mode: an existing `Console` sitting at its own defaults (`BackQuote` / 1000) is adopted **and** configured (`F10` / 4242), one is still created and configured when absent, and re-running setup adds no duplicate | `MainController.cs:517`, `MainController.cs:721`, `MainController.cs:746` | **fixed 2026-08-14** (v0.48.0) |
| H3 | `SetupTextureCacheManager` adds the component to `go` then reads it back with `GetComponent<>()` on `this`. **The defect was real and the fix stands; the reachability claim was wrong, and is corrected here (2026-08-13).** This used to read "Prefab has no `TextureCacheManager`, so this was the **only** path ⇒ `Core.main.TextureCacheManager` was **always null**" - reasoning from `Core.prefab`, which nothing referenced and which is now deleted (M74). `CoreSetup.unity` has carried a `TextureCacheManager` at `Core/MainController/TextureCacheManager` since the initial import (`3bb0a61`), and the pre-fix code adopted it with `GetComponentInChildren` before ever reaching the buggy `GetComponent<>()` line - so in the shipped scene the create path did **not** run and the reference was **not** null. It was live only for a project that removed the scene component. Third entry found resting on that prefab, after M74 and H2 | `MainController.cs` `SetupTextureCacheManager` | **fixed 2026-08-06** (v0.2.0) |
| H4 | Texture cache never `Destroy`s textures — `ClearCache` only dropped references, leaking all native/GPU memory. Plus a throw-away `new Texture2D(128,128)` orphaned per successful download, `UnityWebRequest` never disposed, a repeated `Resources.Load` per failure, and — the one that actually bit the runtime path — `TextureCacheLoader` defaulting `cacheIfNotAvailable: false`, so every load of an uncached image produced a texture nothing owned | `TextureCacheManager.cs`, `TextureCacheLoader.cs:29-32` | **fixed 2026-08-06** (v0.2.0) |
| H5 | `MergeWithDefaults` iterated `GetProperties()`; every settings class uses public **fields**, so the loop body never executed once and config patching was a **total silent no-op**. Walking fields instead would not have fixed it: reflection over a deserialized object cannot distinguish "key absent from the operator's file" from "key present and equal to the C# field initializer", and that distinction is the entire point - the shipped `settings.json` value must win for an absent key, not the initializer. Replaced with a Json.NET `JObject.Merge` at the JSON level: shipped file as the baseline, operator's overlaid, recursive, `MergeArrayHandling.Replace` so a deleted array entry stays deleted, `MergeNullValueHandling.Merge` so an explicit null is respected. Also: the file is now only rewritten when the merge actually changed something, and via temp-file + `File.Replace` rather than `WriteAllText` over the live file - the old unconditional truncate-then-write opened a data-loss window on the site's only config on **every boot**. Parsed with `DateParseHandling.None` so a date-shaped string is not silently reformatted. Dropping the `<T>` also removed `ConfigManager.LoadAndPatchJSON<AppSettings>`, one of the framework→`App` references blocking M20 | `ConfigManager.cs` | **fixed 2026-08-10** (v0.9.0) |
| H6 | The entire `ConfigManager` body was `#if (UNITY_STANDALONE_WIN && !UNITY_EDITOR) \|\| DEBUG_MODE`, so no non-Windows player ever had a deployed config written - the platform where it matters *most*, since Android's StreamingAssets is inside the APK and cannot be opened with `File.*` at all.<br><br>**It could not be fixed by deleting the `#if`.** Doing only that would have turned Android from working into a boot hang: `FileManager.LoadStreamingFile` routed on the *platform*, sending **every** path to the URL-only `LoadJsonWeb` - including `persistentDataPath`, an ordinary readable file on Android. With no deployed config ever written, that branch was never taken; seed one and it returns an empty string, which means null `Core.settings`, no `CoreSetupComplete()`, and the C3 preloader hang. **Android worked because H6 was broken.** Both readers route on `path.Contains("://")` now.<br><br>The gap itself was the root cause: the seeder said `UNITY_STANDALONE_WIN && !UNITY_EDITOR` while the reader said `!UNITY_EDITOR && !UNITY_WEBGL`. There is one definition now, `AppPaths.UsesDeployedConfig`, and it is `static readonly` rather than `const` so the guarded bodies still compile on every target instead of vanishing - which is the bug class H6 belonged to. `architect-reviewer` counted the predicate in **four** sites and three spellings before this.<br><br>Also fixed here, all found by the review round: the shipped side is read inline with `File.ReadAllText` when it is a real file and only fetched for a `jar:file://` path - reading it uniformly through `UnityWebRequest` was the first attempt and moved Windows off the synchronous path, silently making **every config patch take effect only after a restart**; `LoadJsonWeb` joined folder and filename with **no separator** (`.../configsettings.json`), which is every StreamingAssets read on Android, so H6 would have been fixed from launch two and still hung on launch one; the seed write is atomic, so an interruption no longer leaves a half-written `settings.json` that `File.Exists` reports as present; both new requests are disposed and have a timeout; and a failed write no longer leaves the merged config orphaned in a `.tmp`.<br><br>**Two places outside `ConfigManager` were armed by this fix and are fixed with it.** `AppDataController` tested `path.StartsWith("/storage/")` before prefixing `file://` on Android - but `persistentDataPath` is only under `/storage/` with Write Permission = External(SDCard), and this project ships `ForceSDCardPermission: 0`, so the real path is `/data/user/0/<pkg>/files` and the test never matched. Same shape as the `FileManager` defect above, same reason it stayed hidden. The iOS twin tested `"/Users/"`, which is a macOS path. Both are scheme tests now. And `SaveAppSettings` was `#if UNITY_WINDOWS \|\| UNITY_STANDALONE`, contradicting its own remarks - so Android would have gained a deployed `appSettings.json` it could read and never write, with the refusal reported through a `[Conditional("DO_LOGS")]` `LogWarning`.<br><br>**Breaking change for branched projects:** `ConfigManagerHelper.CopyFile` (`public static`) is deleted, along with the private `LoadAndPatchJSON`/`ParseJsonFile`. No caller in this repo. A project that reached for `CopyFile` gets a compile error - though only in a Windows-player or `DEBUG_MODE` compile, since `CopyFile` itself sat inside the old Windows `#if`. The replacement is `ConfigManager`'s own seeding, which now covers its case | `ConfigManager.cs`, `FileManager.cs:53,74,131-137`, `AppPaths.cs:98-125`, `AppDataController.cs:180-194,821-832,974-996` | **fixed 2026-08-11** (v0.16.0) |
| H7 | `MainThreadDispatcher.Update` read `actions.Count` **outside the lock** while worker threads enqueued under it. `Queue<T>` is not thread-safe, so a concurrent `Enqueue` resizing the backing array made that undefined behaviour rather than a merely stale count. The loop also re-read `Count` after every invoke, so an action that enqueued another kept the drain alive and froze the frame - an unbounded livelock inside `Update`. And a throwing action exited the loop, so everything behind it waited for the next frame while Unity logged the exception without saying which queued action produced it. Fixed by draining under the lock into a reusable buffer and invoking **outside** it: one uncontended lock per frame, work bounded to whatever was queued when the frame began, and a per-action `try/catch` that names the failing delegate's Method/Target because IL2CPP strips the stack trace. Invoking outside the lock also removes a latent hazard - an action calling `RunOnMainThread` re-entered the lock, which is reentrant and so harmless today, but holding a lock across a caller-supplied callback is how a deadlock gets built later. Review caught that the buffer `Clear()` was not exception-safe: anything escaping the loop (a `ThreadAbort`, which Mono re-raises at the end of a catch) would leave already-invoked actions in it and the next drain would run them **a second time**, so it is now in a `finally`. `RunOnMainThread` also rejects a null action, before the dispatcher lookup, so a caller bug is not reported as a missing component. `performance-reviewer` confirmed the per-frame lock costs ~40ns against the ~0.1-0.5us Unity spends invoking `Update` at all, and that the drain is allocation-free in steady state | `MainThreadDispatcher.cs` `Update`/`RunOnMainThread` | **fixed 2026-08-11** (v0.14.1) |
| H8 | Two defects. **(a)** the GET query was built as `"?" + name + value` per parameter - no `=`, no `&`, no escaping - so two parameters produced `?a1b2`. Not a malformed query so much as not a query: **no GET this framework ever made can have worked**, which is presumably why nothing calls the GET path. Unescaped values were also the injection half - a value containing `&`, `?`, `#` or CRLF rewrote the request. Now built with `=`/`&` separators and `UnityWebRequest.EscapeURL` on both halves, which `security-reviewer` confirmed closes it (`EscapeURL` is `WWWTranscoder.URLEncode`: escapes every byte below 0x20 and above 0x7E plus the forbidden set, space as `+`, correct for a query component). Starting the builder empty also drops the bare trailing `?` a zero-parameter call emitted.<br><br>**(b)** `fileName`/`folderPath`/`localFilePath` were **instance** fields assigned before `yield return www.SendWebRequest()` and read after it, so a second call on the same `Api` cached the first response under the second's filename. Now locals; only `localFilePath` survived, since `folderPath` is derived where it is used and `fileName` only ever existed to build it.<br><br>**Breaking change for branched projects.** `protected SaveAsJsonFile(rawData, folderPath, filename)` → `private SaveAsJsonFile(rawData, filePath)`. The old `filename` argument was **entirely unused**: the method created the directory from `folderPath` but wrote to the instance field, so its signature described something it did not do. Collapsing rather than redefining is deliberate - `architect-reviewer` and `code-reviewer` both made the point that a same-shape signature whose third argument silently changed meaning would still compile in a derived project and quietly write to a CWD-relative path. `LoadJsonFile` went `internal`→`private` in the same commit for the same one-free-break reason. Neither has a caller outside `Api.cs` in this repo | `Api.cs:77-95,60-61,350,428` | **fixed 2026-08-11** (v0.15.0) |
| H9 | `www` was never disposed and there was **no `www.timeout` anywhere** in `Api`, so a server that accepted the connection and then stopped talking hung the call forever and leaked the native handle plus the download buffer on every call. Now wrapped in `using` and given a timeout from a new `ApiSettings.timeout` (default 30, capped at 300 - `security-reviewer` noted the fix was unbounded at the top end, where a mistyped `86400` restores the same hang).<br><br>**The empty-`cookie`-header claim was never the point.** This entry said Unity rejects empty header values, flagged unverified; it is now moot - the header is only set when `sessionCookie` is non-empty. But `silent-failure-hunter` found the actual defect underneath: `if (index > 0)` on `cookie.IndexOf(sessionName)` rejected **0**, which is what `IndexOf` returns when the session cookie is first in `Set-Cookie` - the common case. So `sessionCookie` was never captured at all, the header was permanently empty, and the only diagnostic was a `LogWarning` behind `Api.doLogs`, which ships false. `>= 0` now, and the miss reports at `LogError`.<br><br>Also fixed here, all from the review round: the CSRF token was written to `Player.log` whenever `doLogs` was on (length only now); a zero-byte cache file satisfied the outer `if` and failed the inner one, invoking **neither** `callback` nor `onEnded` so the caller waited forever; `callback`/`onEnded` sat *inside* the `try`, so a caller whose own callback threw was caught here and invoked a second time; `JsonUtility.FromJson` returning null for a body of literally `null` handed that null to the caller instead of the Error model; `Core.notificationManager` was dereferenced unguarded as the *first* statement of the error branch, where an NRE swallowed the whole failure path; and the `if (www != null)` wrapping that branch was unreachable, since the test above it dereferences `www` | `Api.cs:239`, `Api.cs:250`, `WebRequestExtensions.cs:21`, `ApiSettings.cs:40` | **fixed 2026-08-11** (v0.15.0) |
| H10 | `AssetManager` download path, four defects that all ended in the same place - an exception inside `ValidateAndDownloadMedia_IE`, whose **last** statement increments the counter `OnAssetsSorted_IE` waits on, so the chunk dies, the `WaitUntil` never completes and the app sits on the preloader forever (C3's shape). (1) `UnityWebRequest`/`DownloadHandlerFile` never disposed, and `File.Delete` ran **while the handler still held the file open** ⇒ `IOException`. (2) `DateTime.Parse` of `Last-Modified` used `CurrentCulture` against an RFC1123 header, so it could throw `FormatException` on a pt-PT machine - the deployment locale - and `ArgumentNullException` when the header was absent. (3) `(10 * count) / 100` is arithmetically `count / 10`, hitting 0 under 10 assets, and the test runs *after* the increment so `>= 0` cancelled everything on the **first** failure. (4) `File.Delete` unguarded. Fixed: `using` on both requests; the delete moved after disposal and into a never-throwing helper; `TryParseExact("r", InvariantCulture, AssumeUniversal\|AdjustToUniversal)` with a lenient fallback, compared against `GetLastWriteTimeUtc`; failure limit reworked twice more the same day - see M34 for where it ended up. **Found by review, beyond the original entry:** `GetAssetPath`→`Path.IsPathRooted` throws on CMS values with invalid path characters and sat outside every guard; `Directory.CreateDirectory` and the `DownloadHandlerFile` constructor were likewise unguarded; a failed delete left a 0-byte file whose mtime is *now*, which the retry then skipped as "available and updated" and shipped empty (skip now requires `length > 0`); `filesFailedToDownload` was the one counter `OnAssetsSorted_IE` never reset; and the `WaitUntil` used `==` where a late increment from a previous run would overshoot it forever, now `>=`. A single Error-level summary now reports how many assets will be missing at runtime, since the per-asset detail is `Assert`/`LogWarning` and both vanish without `DO_LOGS` | `AssetManager.cs` `ValidateAndDownloadMedia_IE`, `TryDeletePartialFile`, `OnAssetsSorted_IE` | **fixed 2026-08-07** (v0.8.9) |
| H11 | `LocalizedField<T>` (then named `LocalizedField<T>`) derived from `Dictionary<string, T>` and **never used its own hash table**: every lookup ran a `foreach` comparing keys with `EqualsIgnoreCase`. Since that extension is exactly `string.Equals(a, b, StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase)`, the whole fix is to move the same rule into the dictionary's comparer - identical matches and misses, only the cost changes. Verified against **Unity's own Newtonsoft DLL**: `ToObject<...>()` preserves the comparer on the outer *and* the nested dictionaries, and the old scan and the new lookup agree on every probe.<br><br>**This entry was wrong twice, and both errors overstated it.**<br>1. *"enumerator alloc"* - the scan allocated **nothing**. `foreach` over `this` binds to the inherited `Dictionary<K,V>.GetEnumerator()`, which returns a **struct**; there is no box and no GC pressure. But there *is* one boxed enumerator per `GetTranslation` call, one level up and previously unnoticed: `Translations.IsNullOrEmpty` resolved to the `IEnumerable<T>` overload, whose `Any()` takes the enumerator through the interface. Fixed too, with a `Count` test.<br>2. *"O(keys × languages)"* - it is **additive**, O(keys + languages). The outer lookup finds one entry; the inner scan then runs once, over that entry's languages. The only multiplier in the path was the `.ToLower()` bug below, and it was ×2.<br><br>**Measured 153× (247&#160;ms → 2&#160;ms for 50,000 lookups over 500 keys) - but that is an algorithmic figure, not an improvement to any shipped workload.** This repo cannot exhibit the defect at all: `appData.json` carries **one** translation key, and `MultiLangText`'s GUID appears in **no** scene or prefab. The cost is entirely a branched-project concern, and the place it would have shown is `Core.LanguageChangedEvent`, where every subscribed component re-resolves inside one frame.<br><br>**A regression this fix introduced, caught by review before it shipped:** the comparer makes `"EN"` and `"en"` the same key, so `Helper.ParseTranslations`'s `Add` **threw** on a server sending both - and `AppDataController` catches that exception and leaves `_translations` null, so **one duplicated key would have cost the app every string it has**. Now an indexer assignment, which also makes the two parsers agree: the Newtonsoft path already overwrote last-wins.<br><br>**Breaking for branched projects:** `ContainsKey`, `this[key]`, `Remove` and any other inherited member on a `LocalizedField` are now **case-insensitive**. Nothing here uses them; branch code might.<br><br>**Recorded so they are not rediscovered:** `TryGetExact`, which backs the `caseSensitive: true` overloads, is O(n) where the old `ContainsKey` was O(1) - unavoidable without a `TryGetKey`, and of zero magnitude today since nothing passes `true`. And enum `ToString()` boxes on ~10 call sites; priced at roughly 400 boxes (~10&#160;KB) on a language switch, which is not a GC event - considered and deliberately not done | `LocalizedField.cs`, `Translations.cs:27`, `Helper.cs:20-31` | **fixed 2026-08-12** (v0.21.0) |

Their send methods now also report a port name matching nothing, which looped zero times and returned as though it had sent. **Deliberately unchanged:** `LanguagePanel`, `ImageGalleryItem`, `TextPanel`, `MultiLangText`, `RegisterField`, `ScreenControllerBase` - no per-frame message, no null field read by a public method, and `enabled = false` on a screen controller would disable something other code calls into | `ActivityMonitor.cs`, `SerialPortsManager.cs`, `PhidgetsManager.cs` | **fixed 2026-08-10** (v0.12.0) |
| H14 | Secrets committed in `settings.json` and shipped in StreamingAssets on every derived project: plaintext password `"gema"` and a client hostname. **Corrected 2026-08-07:** the 40-char `OneCore.Authentication.key` is a **dummy placeholder, not a live credential** (confirmed by Tiago), so no rotation is needed - the original entry overstated this. `BuildValidator` now fails a **release** build whose shipped `settings.json` still carries the default password (dev builds are exempt, or the template itself could not be built, and the exemption is logged rather than silent). **Closed 2026-08-11 (v0.19.0), and the register's own description of the lockout bug was wrong.** It said `numTries` is *never reset*. It was reset - in `Show()`, on line 36 - and that was the defect, in the opposite direction and worse: three wrong guesses hid the panel, the next long-press reopened it with the counter back at zero, so an attempt limit of 3 cost an attacker one extra gesture and nothing else. Unlimited guessing, by a mechanism the register had recorded backwards.<br><br>**Hashing.** New `PanelPassword` (PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA1, per-password random salt) stored as one self-describing field, `pbkdf2-sha1$&lt;iterations&gt;$&lt;salt&gt;$&lt;hash&gt;` in a new `EditorSettings.passwordHash`. **10,000 iterations, chosen by measurement**: verification is synchronous in a click handler, so its cost is a frozen frame - 10k is 57&#160;ms, 100k is 547&#160;ms, and half a second of dead UI on a kiosk reads as "the button did not work", so the operator clicks again and spends another attempt. The usual 600k+ advice is for an internet-facing credential store; here the threat is **disclosure** - a plaintext `"gema"` committed to a template every client project branches from - and against a five-letter word 10k and 600k are the same answer. The count travels inside the hash, so a site can raise it with no code change.<br><br>**Lockout.** An instance `_lockedUntilRealtime` deadline, which works precisely because `PanelBase.Hide` only fades a `CanvasGroup` - the component is never destroyed, and `Core` is `DontDestroyOnLoad`, so the deadline survives closing the panel *and* the scene load. `maxTries` and `lockoutSeconds` are new settings fields.<br><br>**Plaintext still works, deliberately**, so that updating the framework cannot lock a live site out of its own diagnostics panel. Every comparison against it logs an error, `BuildValidator` warns on every build, and a **release** build now fails on it.<br><br>**Three review findings were regressions in this fix, not pre-existing holes:**<br>1. *The migration instructions disarmed the only hard gate.* `BuildValidator` failed a release build when `password == "gema"`. Telling operators to blank `password` and set `passwordHash` meant hashing `"gema"` passed every check while the panel still accepted it - and 10k PBKDF2 over a four-character dictionary word falls offline in seconds. Now also fails when `passwordHash` verifies the default.<br>2. *`lockoutSeconds: 0` made the lockout a no-op that reported success.* `Mathf.Max(0, ...)` set the deadline to *now*, `_failedTries` had just been reset, so the next click went straight through while the label read "Locked for 0s." and the log claimed a lockout was applied. A 0 is now honoured as "rate limiting off" and said out loud instead of performed.<br>3. *A malformed `passwordHash` was invisible at build time* - the no-credential warning stayed quiet because the field was non-empty, and the panel refused every entry at runtime. `LooksLikeHash` now gates a warning.<br><br>**The migration has a step that is easy to miss and defeats everything:** `Core` prefers a `PlayerPrefs["Settings"]` snapshot over settings.json when one exists, and that snapshot has no `passwordHash` key - so on any machine that ever saved from the settings panel, the old plaintext password keeps working and none of this takes effect. On Android and iOS that snapshot is the only save target. Clearing Player Prefs is part of the migration, and the tool says so.<br><br>**Verified out-of-tree** against the .NET Framework compiler: 20 round-trip and malformed-input cases, then every bound tested at exactly the boundary and one past it in both directions. Those cases are now a **Run self-test** button in the tool, because a hash that silently accepts everything is this register's defect shape. **Not verified:** any non-Windows target, and no real device.<br><br>**Ceiling, stated plainly:** this stops the password being readable at rest. It does not stop anyone who can *write* `settings.json` from replacing the hash, and `MainController`'s Ctrl+Alt+R restart clears the lockout for anyone with a keyboard. It is a real rate limit against a visitor with a touchscreen, which is the threat a kiosk panel has | `PanelPassword.cs`, `PanelPasswordTool.cs`, `EditorSettingsLockScreen.cs:26-52,95-175`, `DebugSettings.cs:20-56`, `CoreSettings.cs:17`, `BuildValidator.cs:191-220,234-285`, `settings.json:5` | **fixed 2026-08-11** (v0.19.0) |

The design question that kept this open turned out not to apply.** The asset download wrote wherever the CMS said: `AppDataSettings.GetAssetPath` returns a rooted value **verbatim**, so an appData entry of `C:\Windows\…` or `//host/share/x` arrived unchanged, and a relative value was concatenated without normalising, so `../` walked out of the root. Live in the shipped config.<br><br>The entry said containment 'belongs in `GetAssetPath`' and needed the asset root settled first. It does not: that method has **six callers and only one writes**. Guarding the resolver would have changed five read sites to fix one write. The guard went at the write instead (`IsInsideAssetRoot`), reusing the cleanup's own `Path.GetFullPath` + `IsUnder` pattern from 200 lines away. Effort was **S**, not M, and no decision was needed.<br><br>Two things the reviews added. **Containment alone was not enough**, because the root can collapse *upwards*: both halves of `assetsPath` are operator-editable with no validation, and with both empty the property yields `<persistentDataPath>/StreamingAssets` - the **parent of the config folder**. The config folder is now refused whatever the root resolves to. And `IsUnder` compared **case-insensitively on every platform**, so on Android and Linux `../ASSETS/x.jpg` escaped a root of `…/assets`; `IsUnder` follows the filesystem now. The two equality checks beside it - the config-folder test and the pre-existing persistentDataPath one - stay case-insensitive everywhere, which over-refuses off Windows rather than under-refusing.<br><br>A refusal is counted apart from a 404 (`refusedAssets`) and does **not** claim the asset will be missing - the five read callers still resolve a rooted value, so a legitimate off-drive media deployment still displays. And the refusal deliberately does not `File.Exists` the refused path, which would have made the guard an existence oracle for any path the CMS names.<br><br>**Ceiling:** `Path.GetFullPath` does not resolve reparse points, so a junction already inside the root still writes where it points. An empty override does not skip *some* setup - it discards the base wholesale, so `Reset()` never ran and the panel never initialised to alpha 0 / non-interactable / non-blocking. It started however the scene left it, which for a prefab saved in the visible state means a language picker on screen and clickable before anything shows it. The Unity console was the only instrument that knew, and it was not consulted until asked. `CLAUDE.md`'s compile-authority rule now names all four assemblies. **`AssetsVO` was never in doubt** - `Positioner` has a serialized field of it.<br><br>**Retiring `Gema` surfaced a duplicate that the namespace split had been hiding, which is the clearest argument for this entry existing.** There were two `DetectLongClick` classes: `Core/Display/ui/utils/` (110 lines, `EnigmaVirtual`, referenced by one scene) and `Core/Utils/` (48 lines, `Gema`, referenced by none). Moving the second into `EnigmaVirtual` produced `CS0101`, which is how it was found at all. And the counts do not reproduce: a regex over declarations gives **47** global framework types and **25** ordinary ones against this entry's 51 and ~30. The conclusion is unaffected; the number was not.)<br><br>**The counts this entry carried were wrong in both directions, and the two reviews of this slice disagreed with each other (14 against 15) because they counted different populations.** Measured with a parser: **51 before, 42 after**, split four ways - **3** `External/` forks left alone, **11** cleanly movable, **22** genuine extension holders where global is correct, and **6** that share a file with an extension method and must be extracted before they can move. The old `~21`/`~30` split does not appear anywhere in that. Note `ArrayExtensions`, `ProcessExtensions` and `WindowUtils` live in `Extensions/` and are named like holders but contain **zero** `this` parameters, so they are cleanly movable - a review classified them the other way from the filename. **Quote this number only with its scope**; bare, it is wrong three ways.<br><br>**A namespace-sensitive binding mechanism was missing from this entry, and it is the one that fails silently: UnityEvent persistent calls.** A `Button.onClick` wired in the Inspector stores `m_TargetAssemblyTypeName`, a **namespace-qualified type-name string** resolved by `Type.GetType` - not the `.meta` GUID that protects an ordinary `MonoBehaviour` reference. Renaming a type's namespace breaks such a call with **no compile error, no `dotnet build` error and nothing in the Unity console**. Measured on this repo: **zero** of the nine appear in any `m_TargetAssemblyTypeName`, so this slice is safe - but a branch's scenes are not in this repo and `TransitionBase` exposes ~20 public void methods (`Play`, `SlideInRight`, `FadeOut(float)`) that are archetypal Inspector targets. Building all four is what found it, which is the rule v0.49.16 earned after shipping a broken editor assembly this way.<br><br>**Verified: 20 assertions**, both directions on all nine - present as `EnigmaVirtual.<T>`, absent globally - plus the two counter-tests above<br><br>**Final slice 2026-08-18 (v0.54.0): the last two movable types moved, and the *cleanly movable* set is now EMPTY.** `OSCCommandType` and `OscCommand` sat outside the `namespace EnigmaVirtual` block in a file whose other half was already inside it - so they needed moving **into** an existing block rather than the file wrapping, which is exactly why the v0.53.0 slice skipped this file rather than forcing its tool onto it. Census **51 -> 27**.<br><br>**What remains is a different task, not more of this one.** 3 `External/` forks stay global deliberately, 21 are genuine extension holders where global is **correct**, and **3** (`Colors`, `RegisterField`, `ValidationType`) share a file with an extension method and would need extracting to their own file first - a refactor with a real diff, not a wrap.<br><br>**Corrected 2026-08-18, found by `architect-reviewer`: this said 20 and 4, counting `ToggleGroupExtension` among the blocked.** It is not blocked, it is a holder - it declares `GetActive(this ToggleGroup)` at `RegisterField.cs:182`, so global is *correct* for it and no extraction would ever change that. Scheduling it was scheduling work that must never be done. `RegisterField` and `ValidationType` are blocked *by* it, which is how the two got conflated. The census table in `overview.md` had it right all along; the `overview.md` v0.54.0 row, this entry and the worklist row all had it wrong.<br><br>**This is the second time, and the recurrence is the finding.** `doc-reviewer` caught the identical misclassification in v0.50.0 and corrected it in five places (`docs/reviews.md`, *A type bucketed as blocked by an extension holder was the holder*). It returned because the v0.53.0 and v0.54.0 slices each **recomputed** the split as the census moved (42 -> 38 -> 29 -> 27) rather than carrying the classification forward, and each recomputation re-derived it from the same filename-shaped assumption. The one site that survived all three recomputations is the only one that states the reason beside the number. **Do not re-derive this split; if you must, the test is whether the type declares a `this` parameter, not what its file is called.** This entry stays `partly fixed` on that basis rather than closing, but nothing further is *cheap*: every remaining type is either correct as it is or needs a decision about splitting a file.<br><br>**One more global/namespaced pair found 2026-08-19, and it is this entry's category rather than a duplicate defect.** `TransformExtensions` is declared twice inside the **same** assembly: global in `Extensions/TransformExtensions.cs:6` and `EnigmaVirtual.TransformExtensions` in `Core/Utils/extensions/TransformExtensions.cs:6`. Not the L22/L23/L27/M118 cross-assembly shape - one assembly, different namespaces, **zero** method-name overlap, and extension-method lookup admits both, so nothing is shadowed. It is two genuine holders that happen to share a name, which is exactly the collision risk this entry exists to describe for a branched project. Census **27 -> 24**.<br><br>**The close was verified filename-blind, which is the one thing this entry's history demanded.** Its recurring failure was bucketing a holder by what its file is called; so the remaining 24 were classified by asking the loaded assembly which types declare a method carrying `ExtensionAttribute` - that *is* the "declares a `this` parameter" test and it cannot be fooled by a filename. **21 declare at least one; 3 declare none**, and those 3 are exactly the `External/` forks (`ComMessage`, `GUIManager`, `UnitySerialPort`). Nothing movable remains, so this closes rather than staying `partly`.<br><br>**The residual collision surface is smaller than 24, and worth stating because that is what this entry exists to describe.** Three of the 21 holders are `internal` - `TransformExtensions`, `UIExtensions`, `WebRequestExtensions` - so they are invisible to `App` and to a branch's own code and **cannot collide with anything a branch declares**. The real surface is 18 public holders plus the 3 forks.<br><br>**Measured: 17 assertions, 0 fail, both directions on every type.** All three moved types resolve as `EnigmaVirtual.X` and no longer resolve globally; both holders still resolve globally and *not* namespaced; `MathExtensions` is still global as the durable sentinel. The only non-indentation difference in `RegisterField.cs` is one line that held a single space and is now empty.<br><br>**A standing hazard the wrap introduces, and the reason to record it here rather than in a comment.** Extension-method lookup from inside `namespace EnigmaVirtual` now searches `EnigmaVirtual.*` **before** global, so `RegisterField.cs`'s `Field.text.IsValidEmail()` and `Group.GetActive()` are resolved by an outward walk that has a new first step. Verified no shadow today - the 7 extension holders inside `EnigmaVirtual.*` declare neither name - but adding an applicable `IsValidEmail` or `GetActive` under `EnigmaVirtual` later would **silently rebind** this file. That is a general property of wrapping a file, not a defect here.<br><br>**The extracted holder was filed in `Extensions/` rather than beside the file it left**, on review: 19 of the 20 other holders live there, and leaving it under `Core/Modules/UI/Forms/` would have put it exactly where the next census does not look - which is this entry's own failure mode, one more time. | L18 | `VideoPlayerControllerBase` blits the video error placeholder with `Resources.Load<Sprite>("Textures/videoDownloadError").texture` - the load result is dereferenced with no null check, so a missing or renamed asset is an `NullReferenceException` on the error path, which is the path least likely to be exercised before a site visit. `videoDownloadError.png` **is** present today, so this is latent rather than live; what makes it worth an id is that the two image sites (`DownloadableImage`, `TextureCacheManager`) both guard this exact case and log at `LogError`, so the codebase already disagrees with itself about it. Found by review during L7. **Not** folded into `AppResources`: single-use, and that class admits only paths with more than one caller<br><br>**Fixed 2026-08-17 (v0.49.12).** The sprite is held in a local, null-checked, and a missing asset reports at `LogError` (`VideoPlayerControllerBase.cs:322`, `VideoPlayerControllerBase.cs:327`) - the same shape the two image sites already used, which is what made this worth an id rather than a shrug. The path became a named `private const` (`VideoPlayerControllerBase.cs:57`) only because the error message has to repeat it and the literal was inside the call it guards; **still not folded into `AppResources`**, which by its own rule admits only paths with more than one caller, and this has one.<br><br>**The message names the consequence rather than the fault**, which is the part worth copying - and the first version named the **wrong** consequence. It said the RenderTexture keeps whatever frame it last held. It does not: `errorReceived` calls `Stop()` before `DisplayError`, and `Stop()` releases the target texture whenever `resetTextureOnStop` is set, which it is by default - and that target texture *is* this RenderTexture. So the surface is **cleared**, and a message promising a frozen frame would send the reader looking for a symptom they do not have (`VideoPlayerControllerBase.cs:339`). Caught by `code-reviewer`, which traced the call order rather than reading the method in isolation.<br><br>**The guard also checks both halves now** (`VideoPlayerControllerBase.cs:327`). It tested only `errorSprite != null`, while the precedent its own comment cites - `DownloadableImage` - tests the sprite *and* its texture. A sprite whose texture reference broke on import loads non-null and would have reached `Graphics.Blit` with a null source, so the guard looked like parity without being it.<br><br>**Verified 2026-08-17: 4 source assertions and 2 measurements.** The measurements are the mechanism, not the fix: `Resources.Load<Sprite>` on the shipped path returns non-null (so this is **latent**, as the entry said) and on a missing path returns **null** rather than throwing - which is exactly why `.texture` on it was an NRE. Invoking `DisplayError` itself needs a `VideoPlayer` with a target texture and the asset actually absent, so the guard's own branch is not exercised here and the test plan says so | `VideoPlayerControllerBase.cs:322`, `VideoPlayerControllerBase.cs:327`, `VideoPlayerControllerBase.cs:57` | **fixed 2026-08-17** (v0.49.12) |
| L19 | **`VideoPlayerControllerBase` applied the extension extraction twice, and the second application threw.** It called `MediaFile.isValidVideoFileType(url.GetFileType())`, but that predicate takes a url and extracts the extension itself. For `"a.mp4"` this survives by luck - `Path.GetExtension(".mp4")` returns `".mp4"`, measured, not assumed - but an **extension-less url**, which is what a live stream is, extracts to `""`, and `GetFileType` throws `ArgumentException` on empty rather than returning false. So the guard threw out of `ValidateSourceAndRender` and its own `DisplayError()` and `onErrorEvent` never ran: the failure path was disabled by the check meant to trigger it. Measured both ways - passing the url returns `false` cleanly. Found by review during L8; fixed by passing `url`. **Live, not dormant**, for any branched project playing a stream url | `VideoPlayerControllerBase.cs:447`, `MediaFile.cs:76` | **fixed 2026-08-14** (v0.49.4) |
| L20 | **The media walk de-duplicates with `List.Contains`, which is O(n^2) over the walk.** Measured in this editor: n=500 2.7ms, n=1000 10.5ms, n=2000 39.6ms, n=5000 **259ms**, against 0.20/0.18/0.33/5.0ms for a `HashSet` dedupe - all in one frame, on the boot path. Two copies: `TextureCacheManager` (**dormant**, the pre-cache stage is gated by `TextureCache.isActive: false`) and `AssetManager` (**live**, `AssetDownload.isActive` ships `true`). Only the live one is worth changing, and only past roughly 1500-2000 media values - below that it is noise, which is why this is Low and not queued. Keep the `List` for order and add a `HashSet` beside it.<br><br>Second clause, same loop: the three `Development.LogIf` calls in the walk build their `string.Format` arguments even with `doLogs` false, because `[Conditional("DO_LOGS")]` strips the call only where `DO_LOGS` is undefined - and it **is** defined for Standalone and Android. Measured 635 ns + 192 B per walked value, ~3ms at n=5000: larger than the allocation L8 removed, in the same loop. Found by `performance-reviewer` during L8<br><br>**Fixed 2026-08-18 (v0.52.2) on the live copy only**, as this entry prescribed. `AddMediaAsset` keeps the `List` for order and gains a `HashSet<string> mediaAssetIndex` beside it; `HashSet.Add` returns `false` when the value was already present, so one call decides both the list add and which line to log, and the two containers cannot drift through this method. `TextureCacheManager`'s copy is untouched - it is dormant behind `TextureCache.isActive: false`, and the entry says only the live one is worth changing.<br><br>**Second clause fixed too.** The two `Development.LogIf(..., string.Format(...))` calls built their arguments on every walked value, because `[Conditional("DO_LOGS")]` strips the call only where `DO_LOGS` is undefined - and it **is** defined for Standalone and Android, the two targets that ship. Hoisted into `if (doLogs) Development.Log(...)`.<br><br>**Measured here 2026-08-18 at n=5000: 15ms against 643ms** for the `List.Contains` shape - and the 15ms *includes* per-call reflection overhead the real path does not pay, so the true gap is wider. That is a larger spread than the register's own 259ms/5ms, which is a reminder that these numbers are machine- and load-dependent and should be re-measured rather than quoted.<br><br>**Verified**: the list holds each value once in first-seen order, and the **counter-test that matters** - index and list hold the same set after a mixed run of new and duplicate values, because two containers for one fact is precisely what can drift | `Assets/01_Scripts/EnigmaVirtual/Data/AssetManager.cs:68,894` | **fixed 2026-08-18** (v0.52.2) |
| L21 | **`WaitForExit()` on the boot thread, unbounded, so one unkillable process freezes startup with an empty log.** `Kill()` is asynchronous, and a process sitting in an uninterruptible kernel wait never dies - so the parameterless `WaitForExit` inside `MainController`'s kill loop blocks `Init()` permanently. The reason that is worse than an ordinary hang: `Core.BootWatchdog_IE` is a **coroutine**, so a blocked main thread stops it running too, and `BOOT STALLED` never fires. The result is a frozen boot with nothing in the log at all, which is the single worst failure shape this framework has - every other hang at least gets reported.<br><br>Pre-existing, and found by `code-reviewer` inside the block the L10 fix had just restructured. Fixed 2026-08-17 (v0.49.12) in the same commit because it is one argument: `WaitForExit(KillWaitMilliseconds)` (`MainController.cs:432`) with a 5s bound (`MainController.cs:162`), and a `LogError` naming the process when it returns false - continuing rather than waiting, since a process that will not die is not a reason to refuse to boot. **Live** on Windows for any project that lists a process in `killProcesses`, which is the one thing this block exists to do | `MainController.cs:432`, `MainController.cs:162` | **fixed 2026-08-17** (v0.49.12) |
| L22 | **The framework carried a diverged fork of the `AssetGenerator` authoring tool - four files, none of them reachable.** `EnigmaVirtual.Positioner`, `EnigmaVirtual.AssetsVO`, `EnigmaVirtual.Assets` and the global `PositionerEditor` in `Assets/Editor/` duplicated four files under `Assets/ThirdPart/AssetGenerator/` (`namespace UIGenerator`), which still ship and still work. The two `Positioner`s had diverged - the framework copy read `assetData.DesignSize` where the tool's copy has a serialized `designSize` field - so this was a **fork**, not a stray copy, and somebody had adapted it deliberately at some point.<br><br>**Why none of it could run.** `Positioner` has no Unity message methods at all - `Start` is commented out, and `SetPosition`/`SetPositionForget` are called only from the inspector (`SetPositionForget` calls `DestroyImmediate`, which is editor-time by nature). So the component did nothing at runtime and was inert without its editor. The editor in turn parses `StreamingAssets/assets.json`, which **does not exist anywhere in this repo and nothing here produces it** - `PositionerEditor` even carried an explicit warn-and-disable path for its absence, written by someone who knew. And the cluster was closed: every reference among the four pointed at another of the four, with **0** scene/prefab GUID references to any of them.<br><br>**Same shape as L17's `DetectLongClick`, hidden by the same mechanism.** Two `[CustomEditor(typeof(Positioner))]` classes coexisted in `Assembly-CSharp-Editor` without colliding because they target *different* `Positioner` types in different namespaces - so nothing in the codebase could report them as duplicates. Retiring `Gema` in v0.49.15 is what put the two trees side by side where the duplication was visible at all.<br><br>**One correction to my own analysis, made before the deletion.** I first reported that `AssetsVO` had a consumer outside the cluster - `AssetGenerator/Editor/AssetList.cs`. It does not: `AssetList.cs` declares `using UIGenerator;` and resolves the tool's **own** `UIGenerator.AssetsVO`. Found by `silent-failure-hunter` 2026-08-19<br><br>**Fixed 2026-08-19 (v0.55.3), and it was FOUR defects rather than three.** `í` now maps to `%ED`. Both `char` overloads are `[Obsolete]` naming their `string` counterparts, per 5b's discriminator that a `public` member a branch can reach earns a deprecation rather than a deletion. `ReplaceDiacritics(char)` returns the character unchanged - it can never be correct, since every value in the table is a three-character percent-encoding and no single `char` represents one, so returning the input is at least honest where `'%'` was not. `RemoveDiacritics(char)` keeps its best-effort first-letter behaviour but no longer throws on the empty transliteration.<br><br>**The fourth defect was found by a counter-test, and it contradicts this entry's own claim that the `string` overloads are correct.** `RemoveDiacritics(string)` decided "was this character mapped?" by asking whether the output string had grown. Those are different questions: the Russian hard and soft signs transliterate to **nothing**, so appending their empty value leaves the length unchanged, the test read it as "no mapping", and the original character was appended anyway. `"ЪъЬь".RemoveDiacritics()` returned `ЪъЬь` where it should return `""`. Now tracked with a `bool` instead of a length comparison. The same pattern in `ReplaceDiacritics(string)` was corrected too - it is currently harmless because no entry in that table maps to empty, which makes it correct by accident rather than by construction.<br><br>**A systematic check of both tables found 21 entries that look wrong and are RIGHT, and that is worth recording so nobody "fixes" them.** `diacriticsChars` is a **Windows-1252 percent-encoder**, not a Unicode codepoint encoder. For U+0000-U+00FF the CP1252 byte equals the codepoint, which is why 103 entries validate against either model - but the 0x80-0x9F block does not: `€` is `%80`, `Œ` is `%8C`, `™` is `%99`, `Ÿ` is `%9F`, and those are correct CP1252 bytes while being nothing like their codepoints. Verified all 21 against Python's `cp1252` codec. Against that model **exactly one** of the 125 entries was wrong, which is the `í` above, and the counter-tests now pin four of the CP1252 block so a future codepoint-shaped "correction" fails loudly.<br><br>**Also measured: the `char` overload of `RemoveDiacritics` is wrong for 37 of 89 entries**, not just the one that threw - 36 values are multi-character transliterations (`ß`->`ss`, `Æ`->`AE`, `Ð`->`Dj`) which a `char` return truncates to the first letter. That is why it is deprecated rather than repaired: the signature cannot express the answer.<br><br>**Verified: 21 assertions, 0 fail**, including counter-tests that `ý` still owns `%FD`, that the four CP1252 entries are unchanged, that a plain ASCII character passes through, that the `string` overloads still handle `ß`->`ss` and `á`->`a`, and that the `char` overload's documented truncation is still what it does. | L36 | **The in-game debug console draws past the right edge of the screen in portrait, because its toolbar has a hard minimum width that exceeds the screen and `GUILayout.Window` silently grows the window to fit it.** Measured live at 1080x1920, `scaleFactor 0.95`: every control in `DrawToolbar` is `ExpandWidth(false)` and the row is padded with seven `GUILayout.Space(50)` plus one `Space(20)`, so its minimum is **1168.7 GUI units** - of which **370, or 32%, is fixed spacing** - against **1096.8** available (`Screen.width / scaleFactor - 2 * margin`). `GUILayout.Window` sizes to the larger of the rect it is given and its content's minimum, so the window became **1194** units wide where 1026 was requested, and `GUI.matrix`'s 0.95 scale put its right edge at `(20 + 1194) * 0.95 =` **1153.3 px on a 1080 px screen - 73.3 px off-screen**. So the window was simultaneously asking to be too small and being drawn too big, and the two errors cancelled enough to look like one vague layout problem. The `scaleFactor >= 1 ? (margin * 2) : 0` ternary was also dead at the shipped 0.95 - no margin was subtracted at all, and the visible inset existed only because `windowX` happens to equal `margin`.<br><br>**Portrait-only, which is why it survived.** At 1920x1080 the available width is 1981 units and the 1168.7 toolbar fits with room to spare. Caught by `doc-reviewer`.). | H17 | The Windows operator shortcuts dereferenced `Core.settings` - via `Development.Assert(Core.settings.System.doLogs, …)` and via the `killExplorer` check inside `OpenConfigFolder`/`OpenInstallFolder`. `Core.settings` is null whenever settings parsing failed (C3), and `Update` is a Unity message that runs regardless of the boot chain, so **the Ctrl+Alt+S escape hatch threw in exactly the scenario it exists to recover from**. Live on Standalone, where `DO_LOGS` is defined and the `Assert` argument is therefore evaluated. Fixed: the three shortcut breadcrumbs are plain `Development.Log` calls, ungated - an operator reaching for a recovery hotkey is precisely what you want logged on a machine whose startup failed, and `doLogs` is unreachable in that state - and `killExplorer` now reads through a fully null-conditional `KillExplorerRequested` defaulting to **false**, since a partial parse can null `System`/`Windows` while `Core.settings` itself survives. **Found beyond the register's scope:** the same deref in `MainController.Awake` on the Android permission path, where it is worse placed - `Awake` ordering between components is undefined, so `Core.settings` is likely still null - fixed the same way; the Android `Awake` dereference is the one part a device boot does check, and it survived the 2026-08-14 run. Sites at `:300`/`:333` were checked and left alone: they sit in `Init`/`OnDestroy`, both gated on `Core.IsSet`. A scan of every Unity message touching `Core.settings` found three more (`SocketManager`, `OneCore`, `ForegroundEnforcer`) that are **not** live - all three are created by `Init()` and so cannot start before `Core.IsSet`; they belong to H13 | `MainController.cs` `Update`, `KillExplorerRequested`, `OpenConfigFolder`, `OpenInstallFolder`, `Awake` | **fixed 2026-08-07** (v0.8.8) |

## Medium

| ID | Issue | Location | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | **Done (v0.26.0).** Four things, and **the headline was the least reachable of them** - worth stating plainly, because the entry sold it the other way round.<br><br>**The recursion is real but latent.** `LoadImage` was an `IEnumerator` whose only `yield` sat *after* its own `TryDequeue()` call, and `StartCoroutine` runs a body synchronously to the first yield - so the whole chain ran inside the original call, one stack frame per item. But **the queue can only exceed one item through a callback that enqueues**: every `Enqueue` calls `TryDequeue`, which drains before returning, so a caller looping over 550 media still never nests deeper than two. Only a chain where showing image N enqueues image N+1 grows the stack, and nothing in this codebase does that. Fixed anyway - a `while` loop is the same work, flat - but it was **not** the reason this entry mattered.<br><br>**The leak is what mattered - and it is the *texture*, not the sprite.** The register said "Sprites never destroyed" and I fixed exactly that, freeing a few hundred bytes per image while leaving the pixels: `DownloadHandlerTexture.GetContent` allocates a `Texture2D` per download, nothing destroyed it, the `UnityWebRequest` was never disposed, and `Resources.UnloadUnusedAssets` never runs on this path. **Megabytes** per image shown, unbounded over a kiosk day. Both reviews caught it, against a comment I had just written claiming the leak was closed. `DownloadableImage` now frees texture and sprite together in one `ReleaseCreated()`, called before a replacement takes their place and on teardown, and the request is in a `using`.<br><br>**Ownership is deliberately narrow.** Only what this component created is tracked: the error placeholder is a shared `Resources` asset, so it never becomes `_createdTexture` - destroying it corrupts it for the session, the same trap `TextureCacheManager` guards against. And `SetImageSprite` is `public` **and `UnityEvent`-bindable with a dragged `Sprite` argument**, so it does *not* take ownership; a private `OnSpriteCreated` is the loader's callback and the only claimant. A project wiring a sprite asset to the public method would otherwise have that asset destroyed on the next load - and destroying an asset is silent in a player.<br><br>**A throwing callback used to kill sprite loading app-wide, permanently.** `activeLoads--` sat after `LoadImage`, and the callback fans out through `onLoadCompleteEvent` into arbitrary project code; one throw pinned the counter at 1 and sent every later `TryDequeue` straight out of the re-entrancy guard. `ImageLoaderThread` is a singleton on `Core`, so that is every image in the app, after one exception, with nothing further logged. Now a `finally`.<br><br>**`SpriteCreateLayerJob` is deleted, not fixed.** It held managed `UnityEngine.Object` references in an `IJobParallelFor` struct, called main-thread-only `Sprite.Create` from a worker, and its unreferenced caller seeded it with `Sprite.Create(null, ...)`. The job system is the wrong tool for a main-thread-only API, so there was nothing to repair.<br><br>**The name stays.** `ImageLoaderThread` is not a thread and cannot become one - `Sprite.Create` is main-thread-only, and cheap anyway, since a sprite is a view onto a texture rather than a copy. Renaming breaks the `CoreSetup.unity` binding for every branched project, so the class documents what it is instead. Also: `LoadImage` now reports a null texture rather than throwing, and `MainController.EnqueueImageLoadThreadPool` guards the `GetComponentInChildren` result, which returned null silently | `ImageLoaderThread.cs:51,92-113`, `DownloadableImage.cs:69`, `DownloadableImage.cs:200`, `DownloadableImage.cs:413`, `MainController.cs:821-829` | **fixed 2026-08-12** (v0.26.0) |
| M2 | **Done (v0.25.0).** Six defects in `TextureCacheLoader`, and the two that mattered most were not the ones logged.<br><br>**The texture is the cache's, not the loader's.** `SetRawImageTexture` wrote `filterMode` onto it and called `Apply()` - on an instance shared with every other loader showing the same url. `Apply()` on a 2048x2048 RGBA32 is a full **16.78 MB** re-upload on the main thread (~3-8ms), and it ran on **cache hits** too, since `GetOrSetTexture2D` invokes the callback synchronously from the dictionary: eight swaps in one frame cost 25-65ms. It could never do what it looked like it was doing - `UnityWebRequestTexture` produces mipmap-free textures and `Apply(true)` cannot add mips after creation - and it **threw** on the error placeholder, which is a non-readable Resources sprite (`ImageDownloadError.png.meta` has `isReadable: 0`). Both lines are gone. `generateMipMaps` was deleted outright as a provable no-op; **`filterMode` was deleted and then put back deprecated**, because a branched project using `Point` for pixel art would have gone silently Bilinear - blur nobody attributes to a framework bump. It now applies nothing and logs an error at startup if it is non-default. Remove it next minor.<br><br>**`fitType` was a serialized field the code assigned to.** The first load rewrote `Both` to `Width` or `Height`, so every later image reused the first one's orientation and the Inspector showed the rewritten value - `case FitType.Both` was unreachable, and `FitType.Fill` had no case at all and silently resized nothing. Resolved into a local now, `Fill` folded in with `Both`.<br><br>**Two the register did not have, both found by review.** (1) The same `fitType` self-assignment is in `DownloadableImage`, and *that* is the copy that ships - `ImageGalleryItem.prefab:221` has `fitType: 2`. Fixed there too, with the unguarded `imageResizer` dereference beside it. (2) The fit maths read `sizeDelta`, which on a **stretch-anchored** rect is an offset from the anchors and reads `(0,0)` - so the comparison was `0 < 0` for every texture and picked `Height` every time. Both files read `rect.size` at resize time now, with a loud guard for a zero-sized container. My first patch reproduced this faithfully and called it byte-for-byte identical, which it was.<br><br>Also: the `Awake` NRE when `rawImage` is unassigned and `useFadeIn` on (the default) is a guarded bail; `Clear()` no longer allocates an undestroyed `Texture2D` per call, using one `HideAndDontSave` 2x2 for the process; the `AddComponent<ImageResizer>()` whose four calls were all commented out is gone (every runtime path of that component is a no-op); public `ResizeTexture` is gated like `Load` and `Clear`; and `"Missing RawImage or invalid texture!"` now names the url and **blanks the image** rather than leaving the previous item's picture on screen at a freshly-reset size | `TextureCacheLoader.cs:152,262,339-359`, `DownloadableImage.cs:582` | **fixed 2026-08-12** (v0.25.0) |

That made the appData-loaded handler - the first moment the owner exists, and the only place a wrong binding can be corrected - unreachable on the path everything ships with. The new diagnostic could not fire there either. The gate now asks the **owner**, not the local field, so the remote path always takes a second look; the two branches stay mutually exclusive, so nothing can subscribe twice or strand handlers.<br><br>**Both `==` comparisons are Unity's overloaded operator on purpose**, confirmed by `code-reviewer` against all four cases: a *destroyed* owner compares equal to null so the existing binding is kept rather than replaced with a dead one, and a destroyed local binding against a live owner rebinds **silently** rather than falsely reporting two managers. Using `ReferenceEquals` on either operand would break the second case.<br><br>**Verified 2026-08-18: 9 runtime assertions and 5 source checks.** The runtime half drives `AdoptOwnedAssetManager` by reflection with a `MainController` owning one manager and the readout bound to another: it rebinds and reports once at `LogError`. Three are counter-tests - the agreeing case is silent, an unbound readout adopts silently, and a **null owner does not clear a good binding**, which is the ordinary remote path. The source checks pin the orderings the runtime cannot see: subtree then scene-wide then create, both resolve sites calling the helper, and the `Init` gate reading the owner with an `else` keeping the branches exclusive. Confirmed from the scene YAML that the shipped `CoreSetup.unity` has exactly one `AssetManager`, active, under `MainController`, so the new rung never runs there and nothing about the shipped boot changes.<br><br>**Residuals, recorded rather than scheduled.** With **two active** managers and neither under `MainController`, the adopt rung takes an arbitrary one - `FindAnyObjectByType` gives no ordering guarantee - and says nothing; the readout still agrees with it, because `CoreSystemUI` adopts the owner, so this is non-determinism about *which* duplicate runs rather than the split this entry is about. And `OnDisable` never removes the five asset handlers - only the completion handler does - so a boot that never finishes the asset phase leaves them attached; both objects live under the same `DontDestroyOnLoad` `Core` root and are destroyed together, which is why that is noted here instead of given an id | `Assets/01_Scripts/EnigmaVirtual/MainController.cs:728`, `Assets/01_Scripts/EnigmaVirtual/Core/Display/ui/core/CoreSystemUI.cs:295,155` | **fixed 2026-08-18** (v0.49.24) |

The catch reaches the same destination directly instead, using the tail's own message so one outcome reads as one string.<br><br>**The shape that leaked through the first guard was the one that landed worst.** Validating the *entry* is an object does not make its `content` one: a `"content": [ ... ]` or `"content": "x"` passed every check and then threw inside `ToObject` - the exit with the least useful message. `content.Type != JTokenType.Object` closes it. Correct JSON came out only when `values` happened to be the final member of the root; put any member after it and you got that too, minus one character. The register listed the greedy `.+` and the odd `-1` as separate observations - they are one defect.<br><br>**No match check.** Any response without a `values` member - an error payload, an HTML error page from a proxy, an empty body - threw out of the `MatchCollection` indexer instead of reporting a missing field. | M13 | `downloadThreads = 0` made `Ceiling(n / 0.0)` infinite, whose cast to `int` is `int.MinValue`, from which `Split` computed **zero chunks** - and every caller read "no chunks" as "finished, nothing to do", so the boot chain proceeded having downloaded nothing, silently. `TextureCacheManager` was already clamped (v0.2.0); `AssetManager` now is too, and `Split` clamps defensively as a backstop. The clamp is **logged at the clamp sites, not inside `Split`**: with both callers clamping first, `Split`'s own guard can never fire, so logging only there would have left an invalid `downloadThreads` completely silent - which was the defect. **The second half is now closed too, and the claim about it was wrong.** It said the `Skip().Take()` enumeration is O(n²/chunkSize) and "only matters for large asset sets". Measured at n=20000 in the editor, that is false on both counts. `Enumerable.Skip` type-tests its argument at runtime: a `List<T>` gets a `ListPartition` that indexes straight to `start`, so the old code was already **O(n)** and ran 0.6ms flat at every chunk size - and both callers pass `List<string>`. The quadratic mode is real but needs a source with **no indexer** (`HashSet<T>` gets an `EnumerablePartition`, which walks: 1.9 / 2.5 / **12.9ms** at chunk sizes 20000 / 2500 / 313) **and** `downloadThreads > 1`, since the shipped `1` makes chunkSize the whole list and `Skip(0)` walks nothing. Individually raised to `LogError`: `ScreenControllerBase` x2 (missing screen was a double-gated `Assert`, then silently redirected), `DownloadableImage` x5 aborts, `UnitySerialPort` x2 write failures (a command the hardware never received). `SerialPortsManager` un-double-gated but left at Warning - an unparseable frame is ordinary line noise. `TextureExtensions.IsReadable`'s bare `catch` narrowed to `UnityException` so a null texture no longer reports as a routine "not readable". `DownloadableImage.Load(string)` also reported a re-entrant call as "Invalid or empty url"; now separated. **Residual:** `Core.notificationManager` resolves from the *scene* (the only place it has ever lived) (`Core > SystemUI > NotificationsPanel`), not `Core.prefab` - a derived project that deletes that panel gets null and those twelve sites would NRE inside their catch.
